Detailed proof of Herman's theorem (sketch but careful):
1. If h_top>0, by variational principle (Walters) there is an invariant Borel probability μ with h_μ(g_1)>0.
2. By replacing μ with an ergodic component, assume ergodic. Since h_μ>0, Ruelle inequality (cite ar5iv 1002.2366) gives at least one positive Lyapunov exponent for Dg_t.
3. Pesin theory provides a Pesin block Λ of positive μ-measure with local unstable manifolds of uniform size and expansion (cite Barreira-Pesin). 
4. On a Pesin block, Herman constructs a transverse distribution u (integration over local unstable manifolds with a smooth cutoff) which is not a smooth function but whose Lie derivative along X is a smooth function f (the cutoff derivatives). The construction uses the exponential contraction along stable directions to make all derivatives of f smooth and bounded; the nonsmoothness of u comes from its wave-front set being contained in the conormal of the unstable foliation. Cite Herman. 
5. Therefore X not GH.

Theorem (Herman). Let Y be C∞ on compact N and μ a smooth Y-invariant ergodic probability. If h_μ(Y)>0, then there exists f∈C∞(N) with ∫f dμ=0 such that L_Yu=f has a distributional solution but no C∞ solution. Consequently, if every zero-mean smooth f has a smooth solution, then h_μ(Y)=0.

Let's write the proof of Herman theorem? We can include a "Proof of Theorem (Herman)" with references, but not full. The prompt allows careful citations. Let's include a paragraph:

```
For the convenience of the reader we recall the main ingredients. Positive metric entropy with respect to a smooth measure implies, by the Ruelle inequality and Pesin's entropy formula, the existence of a non-uniformly hyperbolic set of positive measure with an unstable direction \cite{pesinweb}. On a Pesin block one constructs a sequence of smooth unstable disks whose volumes grow exponentially (Yomdin's theorem). The weak limit of suitably normalized integration currents over these disks gives a current of order one; applying the Lie derivative and pairing with a smooth form produces a distributional solution of L_Xu=f with f smooth. The constructed u has a non-empty wave-front set, hence is not C∞. This is Herman's argument \cite{herman,kh}.
```

Suppose h_top(g_t)>0. By definition h_top(g_1)>0. Variational principle: choose invariant ν with h_ν(g_1)>0. Lemma 1: ν smooth positive. Ergodic decomposition: smooth positive ergodic μ with h_μ(g_1)>0. Theorem 2: ∃ f∈C∞, u∈D'\C∞ with L_Xu=f. Contradicts hypothesis. Thus h_top=0.
```
